[BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Generative: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [Strategic Contact contact-center cost study](https://www.strategiccontact.com/pdf/CC_Cost_WP.pdf); [Klarna AI assistant 2024 announcement](https://www.klarna.com/international/press/klarna-ai-assistant-handles-two-thirds-of-customer-service-chats-in-its-first-month/); [IBM Vodafone TOBi case study](https://www.ibm.com/case-studies/vodafone-tobi); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

### Intent detection

1. **Name + aliases** — Intent detection; aliases include contact reason, utterance classification, reason-code prediction.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is what the customer is trying to accomplish and what business process should handle it. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: support. Outcome owner: chief customer officer, VP support, contact-center leader, CX operations, knowledge leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Labor is the largest support cost: contact-center frontline labor and supervision commonly account for roughly 67% to 76% of operating cost, so deflection, routing, and handle-time reduction have direct labor leverage. [Strategic Contact contact-center cost study](https://www.strategiccontact.com/pdf/CC_Cost_WP.pdf)
5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **message/call/chat**. Decision: **route, invoke workflow, surface article**. Label/outcome: **intent accuracy and containment**. Horizon: **real time**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from millions of real-time or daily decisions in high-volume enterprises; value concentrates in large portfolios, shared services, or high-cost workflows.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: CRM/case management, contact-center platform, telephony, chat, ticketing, knowledge base, product telemetry, QA tools. In practice this data commonly exists in usable form; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to route, invoke workflow, surface article; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are intent accuracy and containment, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — High: data and labels are usually captured in operational systems, and the action can be wired into existing workflow. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
11. **Industry variants** — B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints.
12. **Adjacent/overlapping use cases** — Adjacent to VoC, product analytics, knowledge management, and churn; support use cases own the service workflow and customer-contact outcome.
13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is deployed at scale in many data-mature enterprises: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Klarna’s AI assistant handled 2.3 million conversations, two-thirds of chats, with a 25% drop in repeat inquiries and estimated $40 million 2024 profit improvement; Vodafone reported 99% faster journey-testing turnaround for TOBi. [Klarna AI assistant 2024 announcement](https://www.klarna.com/international/press/klarna-ai-assistant-handles-two-thirds-of-customer-service-chats-in-its-first-month/); [IBM Vodafone TOBi case study](https://www.ibm.com/case-studies/vodafone-tobi)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are hallucinated or outdated answers, weak knowledge governance, missing CRM/ITSM writeback, poor escalation design, and measuring deflection instead of solved issues; NIST stresses mapping, measuring, and managing AI risks across lifecycle. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — NLP: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [Strategic Contact contact-center cost study](https://www.strategiccontact.com/pdf/CC_Cost_WP.pdf); [Klarna AI assistant 2024 announcement](https://www.klarna.com/international/press/klarna-ai-assistant-handles-two-thirds-of-customer-service-chats-in-its-first-month/); [IBM Vodafone TOBi case study](https://www.ibm.com/case-studies/vodafone-tobi); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

### Agent assist

1. **Name + aliases** — Agent assist; aliases include copilot, knowledge assist, real-time guidance.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is what guidance, answer, compliance cue, or next step should be shown to an agent during service work. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: support. Outcome owner: chief customer officer, VP support, contact-center leader, CX operations, knowledge leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Labor is the largest support cost: contact-center frontline labor and supervision commonly account for roughly 67% to 76% of operating cost, so deflection, routing, and handle-time reduction have direct labor leverage. [Strategic Contact contact-center cost study](https://www.strategiccontact.com/pdf/CC_Cost_WP.pdf)
5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **agent-customer interaction**. Decision: **surface article, script, next step, summary**. Label/outcome: **AHT, FCR, compliance, CSAT**. Horizon: **real time**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from millions of real-time or daily decisions in high-volume enterprises; value concentrates where the entity volume and loss/revenue per decision are both high.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: CRM/case management, contact-center platform, telephony, chat, ticketing, knowledge base, product telemetry, QA tools. In practice this data often exists but needs cleaning; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to surface article, script, next step, summary; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are AHT, FCR, compliance, CSAT, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — Medium-high: data usually exists but requires integration, standard definitions, and human-in-the-loop workflow. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
11. **Industry variants** — B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints.
12. **Adjacent/overlapping use cases** — Adjacent to VoC, product analytics, knowledge management, and churn; support use cases own the service workflow and customer-contact outcome.
13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is common in scaled programs and advanced pilots: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Klarna’s AI assistant handled 2.3 million conversations, two-thirds of chats, with a 25% drop in repeat inquiries and estimated $40 million 2024 profit improvement; Vodafone reported 99% faster journey-testing turnaround for TOBi. [Klarna AI assistant 2024 announcement](https://www.klarna.com/international/press/klarna-ai-assistant-handles-two-thirds-of-customer-service-chats-in-its-first-month/); [IBM Vodafone TOBi case study](https://www.ibm.com/case-studies/vodafone-tobi)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are hallucinated or outdated answers, weak knowledge governance, missing CRM/ITSM writeback, poor escalation design, and measuring deflection instead of solved issues; NIST stresses mapping, measuring, and managing AI risks across lifecycle. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Retrieval: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [Strategic Contact contact-center cost study](https://www.strategiccontact.com/pdf/CC_Cost_WP.pdf); [Klarna AI assistant 2024 announcement](https://www.klarna.com/international/press/klarna-ai-assistant-handles-two-thirds-of-customer-service-chats-in-its-first-month/); [IBM Vodafone TOBi case study](https://www.ibm.com/case-studies/vodafone-tobi); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

### Proactive care

1. **Name + aliases** — Proactive care; aliases include proactive support, issue prediction, service recovery.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is which customers will experience a problem before they contact support. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: support. Outcome owner: chief customer officer, VP support, contact-center leader, CX operations, knowledge leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Labor is the largest support cost: contact-center frontline labor and supervision commonly account for roughly 67% to 76% of operating cost, so deflection, routing, and handle-time reduction have direct labor leverage. [Strategic Contact contact-center cost study](https://www.strategiccontact.com/pdf/CC_Cost_WP.pdf)
5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **customer/account/device/order**. Decision: **notify, fix, credit, dispatch, suppress bad experience**. Label/outcome: **inbound volume, NPS, churn, failure rate**. Horizon: **hours to weeks**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from hundreds to thousands of recurring decisions; value concentrates in large portfolios, shared services, or high-cost workflows.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: CRM/case management, contact-center platform, telephony, chat, ticketing, knowledge base, product telemetry, QA tools. In practice this data exists unevenly and usually needs standardization; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to notify, fix, credit, dispatch, suppress bad experience; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are inbound volume, NPS, churn, failure rate, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — Medium: labels, causality, process standardization, or integration are often the bottleneck. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
11. **Industry variants** — B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints.
12. **Adjacent/overlapping use cases** — Adjacent to VoC, product analytics, knowledge management, and churn; support use cases own the service workflow and customer-contact outcome.
13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is common in pilots but less consistently scaled: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Klarna’s AI assistant handled 2.3 million conversations, two-thirds of chats, with a 25% drop in repeat inquiries and estimated $40 million 2024 profit improvement; Vodafone reported 99% faster journey-testing turnaround for TOBi. [Klarna AI assistant 2024 announcement](https://www.klarna.com/international/press/klarna-ai-assistant-handles-two-thirds-of-customer-service-chats-in-its-first-month/); [IBM Vodafone TOBi case study](https://www.ibm.com/case-studies/vodafone-tobi)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are hallucinated or outdated answers, weak knowledge governance, missing CRM/ITSM writeback, poor escalation design, and measuring deflection instead of solved issues; NIST stresses mapping, measuring, and managing AI risks across lifecycle. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Prediction: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.

[Klarna AI assistant 2024 announcement](https://www.klarna.com/international/press/klarna-ai-assistant-handles-two-thirds-of-customer-service-chats-in-its-first-month/); [IBM Vodafone TOBi case study](https://www.ibm.com/case-studies/vodafone-tobi)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are hallucinated or outdated answers, weak knowledge governance, missing CRM/ITSM writeback, poor escalation design, and measuring deflection instead of solved issues; NIST stresses mapping, measuring, and managing AI risks across lifecycle. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Classification: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [Strategic Contact contact-center cost study](https://www.strategiccontact.com/pdf/CC_Cost_WP.pdf); [Klarna AI assistant 2024 announcement](https://www.klarna.com/international/press/klarna-ai-assistant-handles-two-thirds-of-customer-service-chats-in-its-first-month/); [IBM Vodafone TOBi case study](https://www.ibm.com/case-studies/vodafone-tobi); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

### Knowledge-base retrieval

1. **Name + aliases** — Knowledge-base retrieval; aliases include enterprise search, RAG, answer retrieval, article recommendation.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is which authoritative knowledge should be retrieved for a user’s question or process. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: support. Outcome owner: chief customer officer, VP support, contact-center leader, CX operations, knowledge leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Labor is the largest support cost: contact-center frontline labor and supervision commonly account for roughly 67% to 76% of operating cost, so deflection, routing, and handle-time reduction have direct labor leverage. [Strategic Contact contact-center cost study](https://www.strategiccontact.com/pdf/CC_Cost_WP.pdf)
5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **query/ticket/article**. Decision: **answer, cite, recommend, deflect**. Label/outcome: **deflection, answer accuracy, handle time**. Horizon: **real time**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from millions of real-time or daily decisions in high-volume enterprises; value concentrates where the entity volume and loss/revenue per decision are both high.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: CRM/case management, contact-center platform, telephony, chat, ticketing, knowledge base, product telemetry, QA tools. In practice this data exists unevenly and usually needs standardization; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to answer, cite, recommend, deflect; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are deflection, answer accuracy, handle time, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — Medium: labels, causality, process standardization, or integration are often the bottleneck. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
11. **Industry variants** — B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints.
12. **Adjacent/overlapping use cases** — Adjacent to VoC, product analytics, knowledge management, and churn; support use cases own the service workflow and customer-contact outcome.
13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is common in pilots but less consistently scaled: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Klarna’s AI assistant handled 2.3 million conversations, two-thirds of chats, with a 25% drop in repeat inquiries and estimated $40 million 2024 profit improvement; Vodafone reported 99% faster journey-testing turnaround for TOBi. [Klarna AI assistant 2024 announcement](https://www.klarna.com/international/press/klarna-ai-assistant-handles-two-thirds-of-customer-service-chats-in-its-first-month/); [IBM Vodafone TOBi case study](https://www.ibm.com/case-studies/vodafone-tobi)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are hallucinated or outdated answers, weak knowledge governance, missing CRM/ITSM writeback, poor escalation design, and measuring deflection instead of solved issues; NIST stresses mapping, measuring, and managing AI risks across lifecycle. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Retrieval: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [Strategic Contact contact-center cost study](https://www.strategiccontact.com/pdf/CC_Cost_WP.pdf); [Klarna AI assistant 2024 announcement](https://www.klarna.com/international/press/klarna-ai-assistant-handles-two-thirds-of-customer-service-chats-in-its-first-month/); [IBM Vodafone TOBi case study](https://www.ibm.com/case-studies/vodafone-tobi); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

## C. Marketing

### Audience segmentation

1. **Name + aliases** — Audience segmentation; aliases include microsegmentation, clustering, personas, cohorting.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is which customers or prospects behave similarly enough to target or serve as a group. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: marketing. Outcome owner: CMO, growth marketing, lifecycle marketing, performance marketing, brand/content leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Marketing optimization matters because the Fall 2024 CMO Survey placed marketing budgets at 7.7% of company revenue and projected 12.7% digital-marketing-spend growth over the next 12 months. [The CMO Survey Fall 2024](https://cmosurvey.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/The_CMO_Survey-Highlights_and_Insights_Report-Fall_2024.pdf)
5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **customer/prospect/household**. Decision: **target, personalize, suppress, budget**. Label/outcome: **response, margin, retention**. Horizon: **campaign cycle**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from millions of real-time or daily decisions in high-volume enterprises; value concentrates in large portfolios, shared services, or high-cost workflows.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: CDP, DMP, ad platforms, web analytics, email/SMS platforms, CRM, consent management, data clean rooms. In practice this data often exists but needs cleaning; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to target, personalize, suppress, budget; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are response, margin, retention, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — Medium-high: data usually exists but requires integration, standard definitions, and human-in-the-loop workflow. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
11. **Industry variants** — B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints.
12. **Adjacent/overlapping use cases** — Adjacent to sales and personalization; marketing owns spend allocation, audience/contact policy, and message/creative performance.
13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is common in scaled programs and advanced pilots: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Carrefour reported +21% LTV ROAS and a target audience with 3x more annual purchases and 25% higher cart revenue; McKinsey reported next-best-experience programs can raise revenue 5% to 8%. [Google/Carrefour LTV case study](https://services.google.com/fh/files/blogs/carrefour_case_study.pdf); [McKinsey next-best-experience case examples](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/growth-marketing-and-sales/our-insights/next-best-experience-how-ai-can-power-every-customer-interaction)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are selection bias, attribution confounding, channel silos, creative fatigue, bad incrementality measurement, and consent limitations; experiments or geo-holdouts are needed because platform-reported ROAS often overstates causal lift. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Clustering: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [The CMO Survey Fall 2024](https://cmosurvey.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/The_CMO_Survey-Highlights_and_Insights_Report-Fall_2024.pdf); [Google/Carrefour LTV case study](https://services.google.com/fh/files/blogs/carrefour_case_study.pdf); [McKinsey next-best-experience case examples](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/growth-marketing-and-sales/our-insights/next-best-experience-how-ai-can-power-every-customer-interaction); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

### Campaign response modeling

1. **Name + aliases** — Campaign response modeling; aliases include propensity modeling, uplift modeling, response scoring.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is who will respond incrementally to a campaign, channel, message, or offer. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: marketing. Outcome owner: CMO, growth marketing, lifecycle marketing, performance marketing, brand/content leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Marketing optimization matters because the Fall 2024 CMO Survey placed marketing budgets at 7.7% of company revenue and projected 12.7% digital-marketing-spend growth over the next 12 months. 5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **customer/prospect/impression**. Decision: **select audience, bid, send, suppress**. Label/outcome: **incremental response and ROI**. Horizon: **campaign cycle**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from thousands to hundreds of thousands of daily or weekly decisions; value concentrates in large portfolios, shared services, or high-cost workflows.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: CDP, DMP, ad platforms, web analytics, email/SMS platforms, CRM, consent management, data clean rooms. In practice this data often exists but needs cleaning; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to select audience, bid, send, suppress; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are incremental response and ROI, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — Medium-high: data usually exists but requires integration, standard definitions, and human-in-the-loop workflow. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. 11. **Industry variants** — B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints.

[Google/Carrefour LTV case study](https://services.google.com/fh/files/blogs/carrefour_case_study.pdf); [McKinsey next-best-experience case examples](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/growth-marketing-and-sales/our-insights/next-best-experience-how-ai-can-power-every-customer-interaction)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are selection bias, attribution confounding, channel silos, creative fatigue, bad incrementality measurement, and consent limitations; experiments or geo-holdouts are needed because platform-reported ROAS often overstates causal lift. ### Attribution & marketing-mix modeling

1. **Name + aliases** — Attribution & marketing-mix modeling; aliases include MMM, MTA, incrementality, media optimization.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is how much each channel or tactic contributes to outcomes and where the next budget should go. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: marketing. Outcome owner: CMO, growth marketing, lifecycle marketing, performance marketing, brand/content leader.

6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from hundreds to thousands of recurring decisions; value concentrates where the entity volume and loss/revenue per decision are both high.

Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are incremental sales, CAC, ROI, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.

13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is common in scaled programs and advanced pilots: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Carrefour reported +21% LTV ROAS and a target audience with 3x more annual purchases and 25% higher cart revenue; McKinsey reported next-best-experience programs can raise revenue 5% to 8%. [Google/Carrefour LTV case study](https://services.google.com/fh/files/blogs/carrefour_case_study.pdf); [McKinsey next-best-experience case examples](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/growth-marketing-and-sales/our-insights/next-best-experience-how-ai-can-power-every-customer-interaction)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are selection bias, attribution confounding, channel silos, creative fatigue, bad incrementality measurement, and consent limitations; experiments or geo-holdouts are needed because platform-reported ROAS often overstates causal lift. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Generative: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [The CMO Survey Fall 2024](https://cmosurvey.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/The_CMO_Survey-Highlights_and_Insights_Report-Fall_2024.pdf); [Google/Carrefour LTV case study](https://services.google.com/fh/files/blogs/carrefour_case_study.pdf); [McKinsey next-best-experience case examples](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/growth-marketing-and-sales/our-insights/next-best-experience-how-ai-can-power-every-customer-interaction); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

### SEO / keyword intelligence

1. **Name + aliases** — SEO / keyword intelligence; aliases include search intelligence, content gap analysis, SERP monitoring.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is which search intents and content gaps should be targeted to earn organic demand. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: marketing. Outcome owner: CMO, growth marketing, lifecycle marketing, performance marketing, brand/content leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Marketing optimization matters because the Fall 2024 CMO Survey placed marketing budgets at 7.7% of company revenue and projected 12.7% digital-marketing-spend growth over the next 12 months. [The CMO Survey Fall 2024](https://cmosurvey.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/The_CMO_Survey-Highlights_and_Insights_Report-Fall_2024.pdf)
5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **keyword/page/topic**. Decision: **prioritize content, optimize page, update metadata**. Label/outcome: **rank, traffic, conversion**. Horizon: **weekly to monthly**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from thousands to hundreds of thousands of daily or weekly decisions; value concentrates where senior decision latency or scarce expertise matters.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: CDP, DMP, ad platforms, web analytics, email/SMS platforms, CRM, consent management, data clean rooms. In practice this data often exists but needs cleaning; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to prioritize content, optimize page, update metadata; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are rank, traffic, conversion, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — Medium-high: data usually exists but requires integration, standard definitions, and human-in-the-loop workflow. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
11. **Industry variants** — B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints.
12. **Adjacent/overlapping use cases** — Adjacent to sales and personalization; marketing owns spend allocation, audience/contact policy, and message/creative performance.
13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is common in scaled programs and advanced pilots: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Carrefour reported +21% LTV ROAS and a target audience with 3x more annual purchases and 25% higher cart revenue; McKinsey reported next-best-experience programs can raise revenue 5% to 8%. [Google/Carrefour LTV case study](https://services.google.com/fh/files/blogs/carrefour_case_study.pdf); [McKinsey next-best-experience case examples](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/growth-marketing-and-sales/our-insights/next-best-experience-how-ai-can-power-every-customer-interaction)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are selection bias, attribution confounding, channel silos, creative fatigue, bad incrementality measurement, and consent limitations; experiments or geo-holdouts are needed because platform-reported ROAS often overstates causal lift. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — NLP: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [The CMO Survey Fall 2024](https://cmosurvey.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/The_CMO_Survey-Highlights_and_Insights_Report-Fall_2024.pdf); [Google/Carrefour LTV case study](https://services.google.com/fh/files/blogs/carrefour_case_study.pdf); [McKinsey next-best-experience case examples](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/growth-marketing-and-sales/our-insights/next-best-experience-how-ai-can-power-every-customer-interaction); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

### Email send-time / frequency optimization

1. **Name + aliases** — Email send-time / frequency optimization; aliases include contact cadence, fatigue modeling, send-time optimization.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is when and how often to contact a customer without causing fatigue or unsubscribes. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: marketing. Outcome owner: CMO, growth marketing, lifecycle marketing, performance marketing, brand/content leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Marketing optimization matters because the Fall 2024 CMO Survey placed marketing budgets at 7.7% of company revenue and projected 12.7% digital-marketing-spend growth over the next 12 months. [The CMO Survey Fall 2024](https://cmosurvey.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/11/The_CMO_Survey-Highlights_and_Insights_Report-Fall_2024.pdf)
5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **recipient/message/day**. Decision: **send, suppress, throttle, change channel**. Label/outcome: **open, click, conversion, unsubscribe**. Horizon: **daily to campaign**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from millions of real-time or daily decisions in high-volume enterprises; value concentrates where senior decision latency or scarce expertise matters.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: CDP, DMP, ad platforms, web analytics, email/SMS platforms, CRM, consent management, data clean rooms. In practice this data commonly exists in usable form; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to send, suppress, throttle, change channel; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are open, click, conversion, unsubscribe, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — High: data and labels are usually captured in operational systems, and the action can be wired into existing workflow. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
11. **Industry variants** — B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints.
12. **Adjacent/overlapping use cases** — Adjacent to sales and personalization; marketing owns spend allocation, audience/contact policy, and message/creative performance.
13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is deployed at scale in many data-mature enterprises: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Carrefour reported +21% LTV ROAS and a target audience with 3x more annual purchases and 25% higher cart revenue; McKinsey reported next-best-experience programs can raise revenue 5% to 8%. [Google/Carrefour LTV case study](https://services.google.com/fh/files/blogs/carrefour_case_study.pdf); [McKinsey next-best-experience case examples](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/growth-marketing-and-sales/our-insights/next-best-experience-how-ai-can-power-every-customer-interaction)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are selection bias, attribution confounding, channel silos, creative fatigue, bad incrementality measurement, and consent limitations; experiments or geo-holdouts are needed because platform-reported ROAS often overstates causal lift. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Optimization: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.

## D. Finance & accounting

### Cash-flow and P&L forecasting

1. **Name + aliases** — Cash-flow and P&L forecasting; aliases include liquidity forecasting, treasury forecast, FP&A forecast.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is what cash, revenue, cost, and margin outcomes will occur under current operating assumptions. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: finance. Outcome owner: CFO, controller, treasurer, FP&A, credit, AP/AR, or shared-services leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Finance automation value comes from working capital, payment risk, and labor: PwC estimated €1.56 trillion of global excess working capital in 2024/25, and AFP found 79% of organizations experienced actual or attempted payments fraud in 2024. [PwC Working Capital Study 2024/25](https://www.pwc.co.uk/services/value-creation/insights/working-capital-study/2024.html); [2025 AFP Payments Fraud and Control Survey](https://www.truist.com/content/dam/truist-bank/us/en/documents/info/cci/2025-afp-payments-fraud-control-survey-report-key-highlights.pdf)
5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **entity/account/business unit/period**. Decision: **fund, hedge, borrow, invest, revise plan**. Label/outcome: **forecast error, liquidity variance**. Horizon: **daily to monthly**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from thousands to hundreds of thousands of daily or weekly decisions; value concentrates where the entity volume and loss/revenue per decision are both high.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: ERP, AP/AR, treasury, TMS, bank statements, expense management, GL, close/reconciliation tools, procurement systems. In practice this data often exists but needs cleaning; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to fund, hedge, borrow, invest, revise plan; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are forecast error, liquidity variance, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — Medium-high: data usually exists but requires integration, standard definitions, and human-in-the-loop workflow. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
11. **Industry variants** — Large enterprises have ERP/AP/AR history; banks and insurers add regulated model governance; smaller firms often lack clean labels and standardized workflows.
12. **Adjacent/overlapping use cases** — Adjacent to procurement, risk, and operations; finance use cases own cash, controls, close, and financial planning outcomes.
13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is common in scaled programs and advanced pilots: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Genpact reported an enterprise AP program reaching 80% touchless processing, while HighRadius reported Staples reduced bad debt by 20% using collections automation. [Genpact 80% touchless AP case study](https://www.genpact.com/case-studies/from-manual-to-80-percent-touchless-accounts-payable-at-enterprise-scale); [HighRadius Staples collections case study](https://www.highradius.com/resources/Radiance/20-reduction-in-bad-debt-staples-collections-automation-journey-with-ai/)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are inconsistent master data, unclear approval authority, exception-heavy processes, audit concerns, and automating broken controls; payment-fraud prevalence and working-capital scale make false positives and control design material. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Forecasting: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [PwC Working Capital Study 2024/25](https://www.pwc.co.uk/services/value-creation/insights/working-capital-study/2024.html); [2025 AFP Payments Fraud and Control Survey](https://www.truist.com/content/dam/truist-bank/us/en/documents/info/cci/2025-afp-payments-fraud-control-survey-report-key-highlights.pdf); [Genpact 80% touchless AP case study](https://www.genpact.com/case-studies/from-manual-to-80-percent-touchless-accounts-payable-at-enterprise-scale); [HighRadius Staples collections case study](https://www.highradius.com/resources/Radiance/20-reduction-in-bad-debt-staples-collections-automation-journey-with-ai/); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

### Collections prioritization & DSO reduction

1. **Name + aliases** — Collections prioritization & DSO reduction; aliases include AR collections scoring, delinquency prioritization.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is which receivables are most likely to pay late and which action will accelerate cash. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: finance. Outcome owner: CFO, controller, treasurer, FP&A, credit, AP/AR, or shared-services leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Finance automation value comes from working capital, payment risk, and labor: PwC estimated €1.56 trillion of global excess working capital in 2024/25, and AFP found 79% of organizations experienced actual or attempted payments fraud in 2024. [PwC Working Capital Study 2024/25](https://www.pwc.co.uk/services/value-creation/insights/working-capital-study/2024.html); [2025 AFP Payments Fraud and Control Survey](https://www.truist.com/content/dam/truist-bank/us/en/documents/info/cci/2025-afp-payments-fraud-control-survey-report-key-highlights.pdf)
5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **invoice/customer/account**. Decision: **call, email, dispute, hold order, escalate**. Label/outcome: **DSO, cash collected, bad debt**. Horizon: **daily**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from millions of real-time or daily decisions in high-volume enterprises; value concentrates where the entity volume and loss/revenue per decision are both high.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: ERP, AP/AR, treasury, TMS, bank statements, expense management, GL, close/reconciliation tools, procurement systems. In practice this data commonly exists in usable form; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to call, email, dispute, hold order, escalate; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are DSO, cash collected, bad debt, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — High: data and labels are usually captured in operational systems, and the action can be wired into existing workflow. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
11. **Industry variants** — Large enterprises have ERP/AP/AR history; banks and insurers add regulated model governance; smaller firms often lack clean labels and standardized workflows.
12. **Adjacent/overlapping use cases** — Adjacent to procurement, risk, and operations; finance use cases own cash, controls, close, and financial planning outcomes.
13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is deployed at scale in many data-mature enterprises: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Genpact reported an enterprise AP program reaching 80% touchless processing, while HighRadius reported Staples reduced bad debt by 20% using collections automation. [Genpact 80% touchless AP case study](https://www.genpact.com/case-studies/from-manual-to-80-percent-touchless-accounts-payable-at-enterprise-scale); [HighRadius Staples collections case study](https://www.highradius.com/resources/Radiance/20-reduction-in-bad-debt-staples-collections-automation-journey-with-ai/)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are inconsistent master data, unclear approval authority, exception-heavy processes, audit concerns, and automating broken controls; payment-fraud prevalence and working-capital scale make false positives and control design material. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Scoring: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [PwC Working Capital Study 2024/25](https://www.pwc.co.uk/services/value-creation/insights/working-capital-study/2024.html); [2025 AFP Payments Fraud and Control Survey](https://www.truist.com/content/dam/truist-bank/us/en/documents/info/cci/2025-afp-payments-fraud-control-survey-report-key-highlights.pdf); [Genpact 80% touchless AP case study](https://www.genpact.com/case-studies/from-manual-to-80-percent-touchless-accounts-payable-at-enterprise-scale); [HighRadius Staples collections case study](https://www.highradius.com/resources/Radiance/20-reduction-in-bad-debt-staples-collections-automation-journey-with-ai/); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

16. **Typical methodology** — Anomaly: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [PwC Working Capital Study 2024/25](https://www.pwc.co.uk/services/value-creation/insights/working-capital-study/2024.html); [2025 AFP Payments Fraud and Control Survey](https://www.truist.com/content/dam/truist-bank/us/en/documents/info/cci/2025-afp-payments-fraud-control-survey-report-key-highlights.pdf); [Genpact 80% touchless AP case study](https://www.genpact.com/case-studies/from-manual-to-80-percent-touchless-accounts-payable-at-enterprise-scale); [HighRadius Staples collections case study](https://www.highradius.com/resources/Radiance/20-reduction-in-bad-debt-staples-collections-automation-journey-with-ai/); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

### Financial-close automation

1. **Name + aliases** — Financial-close automation; aliases include close orchestration, reconciliation automation.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is which close tasks, reconciliations, flux explanations, and support packets can be automated or prioritized. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: finance. Outcome owner: CFO, controller, treasurer, FP&A, credit, AP/AR, or shared-services leader.
4. **Economic pool** — Finance automation value comes from working capital, payment risk, and labor: PwC estimated €1.56 trillion of global excess working capital in 2024/25, and AFP found 79% of organizations experienced actual or attempted payments fraud in 2024. [PwC Working Capital Study 2024/25](https://www.pwc.co.uk/services/value-creation/insights/working-capital-study/2024.html); [2025 AFP Payments Fraud and Control Survey](https://www.truist.com/content/dam/truist-bank/us/en/documents/info/cci/2025-afp-payments-fraud-control-survey-report-key-highlights.pdf)
5. **Decision framing** — Entity: **account/reconciliation/task/entity**. Decision: **reconcile, certify, route, explain variance**. Label/outcome: **days-to-close, rework, audit issues**. Horizon: **monthly/quarterly**.
6. **Decision frequency and scale** — Typical scale ranges from thousands to hundreds of thousands of daily or weekly decisions; value concentrates in large portfolios, shared services, or high-cost workflows.
7. **Data exhaust** — Natural exhaust: ERP, AP/AR, treasury, TMS, bank statements, expense management, GL, close/reconciliation tools, procurement systems. In practice this data often exists but needs cleaning; the hard work is entity resolution, history depth, permissions, and workflow writeback.
8. **Actionability** — A correct prediction or decision enables the owner to reconcile, certify, route, explain variance; actions should run through the system of action already used by the function, such as CRM, ERP, ITSM, WMS/TMS, MES, EHR, HRIS, CLM, SIEM, or a controlled workflow queue.
9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical. Hard KPIs should be operating metrics the function already owns, not model accuracy alone. Primary KPIs are days-to-close, rework, audit issues, plus unit economics, cycle time, exception rate, and risk-adjusted cost where applicable.
10. **Prerequisites & feasibility** — Medium-high: data usually exists but requires integration, standard definitions, and human-in-the-loop workflow. Minimum conditions are a named business owner, two or more cycles of history, stable label definition, governed feature access, workflow integration, and a holdout or experiment design; NIST’s AI RMF frames this as governing, mapping, measuring, and managing risks across the AI lifecycle. [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
11. **Industry variants** — Large enterprises have ERP/AP/AR history; banks and insurers add regulated model governance; smaller firms often lack clean labels and standardized workflows.
12. **Adjacent/overlapping use cases** — Adjacent to procurement, risk, and operations; finance use cases own cash, controls, close, and financial planning outcomes.
13. **Prevalence & adoption** — Prevalence is common in scaled programs and advanced pilots: McKinsey reported 88% of organizations regularly use AI in at least one function in 2025, but only about one-third are scaling AI enterprise-wide; Deloitte’s AI Dossier catalogs 125+ use cases across industry and function. [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html)
14. **Documented results** — Representative results: Genpact reported an enterprise AP program reaching 80% touchless processing, while HighRadius reported Staples reduced bad debt by 20% using collections automation. [Genpact 80% touchless AP case study](https://www.genpact.com/case-studies/from-manual-to-80-percent-touchless-accounts-payable-at-enterprise-scale); [HighRadius Staples collections case study](https://www.highradius.com/resources/Radiance/20-reduction-in-bad-debt-staples-collections-automation-journey-with-ai/)
15. **Failure modes** — Common failures are inconsistent master data, unclear approval authority, exception-heavy processes, audit concerns, and automating broken controls; payment-fraud prevalence and working-capital scale make false positives and control design material. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf)
16. **Typical methodology** — Workflow: reusable data extraction, feature/context construction, model or rules+model decisioning, explanation, monitoring, and workflow execution.
17. **Key references** — [PwC Working Capital Study 2024/25](https://www.pwc.co.uk/services/value-creation/insights/working-capital-study/2024.html); [2025 AFP Payments Fraud and Control Survey](https://www.truist.com/content/dam/truist-bank/us/en/documents/info/cci/2025-afp-payments-fraud-control-survey-report-key-highlights.pdf); [Genpact 80% touchless AP case study](https://www.genpact.com/case-studies/from-manual-to-80-percent-touchless-accounts-payable-at-enterprise-scale); [HighRadius Staples collections case study](https://www.highradius.com/resources/Radiance/20-reduction-in-bad-debt-staples-collections-automation-journey-with-ai/); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai); [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [NIST AI RMF 1.0](https://nvlpubs.nist.gov/nistpubs/ai/nist.ai.100-1.pdf).

### FP&A scenario modeling

1. **Name + aliases** — FP&A scenario modeling; aliases include driver-based planning, what-if forecasting, variance scenarios.
2. **Problem statement** — The business problem is how financial outcomes change under alternative demand, cost, capacity, price, and macro assumptions. The use case is defined at the problem layer: the enterprise must decide what to do about a recurring entity before the outcome is fully known.
3. **Who has it** — Industries: any sector where this entity and outcome recur at scale, with material variants noted below. Business function: finance. Outcome owner: CFO, controller, treasurer, FP&A, credit, AP/AR, or shared-services leader.

9. **Measurability** — Impact should be proven with A/B tests, randomized or quasi-experimental holdouts, champion/challenger monitoring, or before/after analysis only when randomized proof is impractical.
