## Cross-cutting syntheses

### 1. Master taxonomy
| Use case | Business function | Industries / variants | Entity / decision | Economic pool | Typical methodology |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Churn prediction & retention | Customer / revenue / customer | SaaS defines churn at account or logo renewal; telecom and banking define it at subscriber/account level; retail often treats it as repeat purchase and spend decay; marketplaces treat it as merchant or buyer dormancy | account/subscriber/merchant → retain, save, recover, or let churn | customer | Classification |
| Customer lifetime value | Customer / revenue / customer | SaaS defines churn at account or logo renewal; telecom and banking define it at subscriber/account level; retail often treats it as repeat purchase and spend decay; marketplaces treat it as merchant or buyer dormancy | customer/account/household → acquire, invest, tier, suppress, or prioritize | customer | Regression |
| Next-best-action / next-best-offer | Customer / revenue / customer | SaaS defines churn at account or logo renewal; telecom and banking define it at subscriber/account level; retail often treats it as repeat purchase and spend decay; marketplaces treat it as merchant or buyer dormancy | customer/account/session → present action/offer or suppress contact | customer | Optimization |
| Personalization & product recommendations | Customer / revenue / customer | SaaS defines churn at account or logo renewal; telecom and banking define it at subscriber/account level; retail often treats it as repeat purchase and spend decay; marketplaces treat it as merchant or buyer dormancy | visitor/session/customer/item → rank and display products/content | customer | Ranking |
| Lead scoring | Customer / revenue / customer | SaaS defines churn at account or logo renewal; telecom and banking define it at subscriber/account level; retail often treats it as repeat purchase and spend decay; marketplaces treat it as merchant or buyer dormancy | lead/account/contact → route, prioritize, nurture, or disqualify | customer | Scoring |
| Opportunity / win prediction | Customer / revenue / sales | Executive analytics cuts across functions; the material variant is whether it is used for real operating decisions or only static reporting | opportunity/deal → coach, inspect, discount, advance, or de-prioritize | sales | Scoring |
| Sales forecasting | Customer / revenue / sales | Executive analytics cuts across functions; the material variant is whether it is used for real operating decisions or only static reporting | territory/product/rep/opportunity aggregate → commit forecast, capacity, hiring, spend | sales | Forecasting |
| Pricing & discount optimization | Customer / revenue / customer | SaaS defines churn at account or logo renewal; telecom and banking define it at subscriber/account level; retail often treats it as repeat purchase and spend decay; marketplaces treat it as merchant or buyer dormancy | SKU/offer/deal/customer → set price, discount, guardrail, or approval | customer | Optimization |
| Promotion effectiveness | Customer / revenue / marketing | B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints | promotion/customer/SKU/store → fund, change, stop, or reallocate promotion | marketing | Causal |
| Market-basket / cross-sell | Customer / revenue / customer | SaaS defines churn at account or logo renewal; telecom and banking define it at subscriber/account level; retail often treats it as repeat purchase and spend decay; marketplaces treat it as merchant or buyer dormancy | customer/basket/SKU/account → recommend, bundle, sequence, or prompt seller | customer | Association |
| Customer feedback & sentiment analysis | Customer / revenue / support | B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints | review/ticket/call/social post → route issue, fix product, message recovery | support | NLP |
| Voice-of-customer mining | Customer / revenue / support | B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints | utterance/document/theme/customer journey → open defect, create KB, change policy, prioritize roadmap | support | NLP |
| Ticket triage & routing | Customer service & support / support | B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints | ticket/case/message → route, prioritize, escalate, merge | support | Classification |
| Response drafting / resolution automation | Customer service & support / support | B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints | conversation/ticket/intent → draft, send, automate, handoff | support | Generative |
| Intent detection | Customer service & support / support | B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints | message/call/chat → route, invoke workflow, surface article | support | NLP |
| Agent assist | Customer service & support / support | B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints | agent-customer interaction → surface article, script, next step, summary | support | Retrieval |
| Proactive care | Customer service & support / support | B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints | customer/account/device/order → notify, fix, credit, dispatch, suppress bad experience | support | Prediction |
| Call-center analytics | Customer service & support / support | B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints | call/transcript/agent/customer → coach, audit, improve script, tag issue | support | NLP |
| CSAT / escalation prediction | Customer service & support / support | B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints | ticket/customer/interaction → escalate, assign senior agent, service recovery | support | Classification |
| Knowledge-base retrieval | Customer service & support / support | B2C support is high-volume and real-time; B2B support is lower-volume but higher-value and SLA-driven; regulated support adds auditability and compliance constraints | query/ticket/article → answer, cite, recommend, deflect | support | Retrieval |
| Audience segmentation | Marketing / marketing | B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints | customer/prospect/household → target, personalize, suppress, budget | marketing | Clustering |
| Campaign response modeling | Marketing / marketing | B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints | customer/prospect/impression → select audience, bid, send, suppress | marketing | Causal |
| Attribution & marketing-mix modeling | Marketing / marketing | B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints | channel/campaign/geo/time period → reallocate budget, set spend, optimize mix | marketing | Causal |
| Content generation & optimization | Marketing / marketing | B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints | asset/page/email/ad → draft, test, localize, publish | marketing | Generative |
| SEO / keyword intelligence | Marketing / marketing | B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints | keyword/page/topic → prioritize content, optimize page, update metadata | marketing | NLP |
| Creative testing | Marketing / marketing | B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints | creative/impression/audience → serve, pause, iterate, localize | marketing | Bandit |
| Email send-time / frequency optimization | Marketing / marketing | B2C variants optimize impressions, channels, and products; B2B variants optimize accounts, buying committees, and pipeline; regulated industries add consent and fairness constraints | recipient/message/day → send, suppress, throttle, change channel | marketing | Optimization |
| Cash-flow and P&L forecasting | Finance & accounting / finance | Large enterprises have ERP/AP/AR history; banks and insurers add regulated model governance; smaller firms often lack clean labels and standardized workflows | entity/account/business unit/period → fund, hedge, borrow, invest, revise plan | finance | Forecasting |
| Collections prioritization & DSO reduction | Finance & accounting / finance | Large enterprises have ERP/AP/AR history; banks and insurers add regulated model governance; smaller firms often lack clean labels and standardized workflows | invoice/customer/account → call, email, dispute, hold order, escalate | finance | Scoring |
| AP/AR exception handling | Finance & accounting / finance | Large enterprises have ERP/AP/AR history; banks and insurers add regulated model governance; smaller firms often lack clean labels and standardized workflows | invoice/payment/remittance → route, resolve, match, approve | finance | Classification |

### 2. Value ranking
Ranking heuristic: value score = 35% economic-pool magnitude, 25% decision frequency, 20% actionability/data availability, and 20% measurability. The ranking is directional rather than a universal ROI claim because enterprise value depends on baseline maturity, margin structure, intervention capacity, and change management; BCG’s value-gap findings show that adoption alone is not the same as realized value. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [BCG 2025 AI value-gap study](https://www.bcg.com/publications/2025/are-you-generating-value-from-ai-the-widening-gap)

### 3. Feasibility ranking
Feasibility is higher where enterprises already have entity-level history, labels, and an operational action system; it is lower where sparse labels, causality, regulatory proof, physical-world telemetry, or fragmented documents dominate.

### 6. Prevalence reality-check
Use cases deployed at scale tend to be prediction, routing, search/retrieval, classification, and optimization problems with stable workflows: churn, personalization, fraud, support routing/assist, AP automation, demand forecasting, replenishment, predictive maintenance, AIOps, cybersecurity detection, and code assistance. This aligns with broad adoption data: IBM found 42% of enterprises had actively deployed AI in late 2023 and another 40% were exploring it, while McKinsey found 88% regular use by 2025 but only one-third scaling enterprise-wide. [IBM Global AI Adoption Index 2023](https://filecache.mediaroom.com/mr5mr_ibmspgi/179414/download/IBM%20Global%20AI%20Adoption%20Index%20Report%20Dec.%202023.pdf); [McKinsey State of AI 2025](https://www.mckinsey.com/capabilities/quantumblack/our-insights/the-state-of-ai)
Use cases still mostly piloted include agentic cross-functional reporting, regulatory-change monitoring, model-risk automation, strategy synthesis, broad digital twins, clinical generative documentation at scale, and M&A screening, because they require governance, trust, and senior workflow redesign. Deloitte’s catalog of 125+ use cases shows breadth, but BCG’s evidence shows only a minority of companies turn use cases into tangible scaled value. [Deloitte AI Dossier](https://www.deloitte.com/us/en/what-we-do/capabilities/applied-artificial-intelligence/content/ai-use-cases.html); [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value)

### 7. Failure-pattern synthesis
BCG’s 2024 study found that 74% of companies had yet to show tangible AI value, and MIT Sloan/BCG found only 10% obtained significant financial benefits from AI in 2020; recurring failure patterns therefore need to be managed as operating-model risks, not just model risks. [BCG 2024 AI adoption study](https://www.bcg.com/press/24october2024-ai-adoption-in-2024-74-of-companies-struggle-to-achieve-and-scale-value); [MIT Sloan Management Review and BCG 2020 study](https://sloanreview.mit.edu/projects/expanding-ais-impact-with-organizational-learning/)
| Failure pattern | What it looks like | Most susceptible use cases |
|---|---|---|
| No action owner | Model outputs do not change a funded decision or workflow | Executive reporting, churn, HR attrition, anomaly detection |
| Bad label definition | Outcome is delayed, biased, inconsistent, or not tied to the intervention | Fraud, churn, HR, sales forecasting, readmission |
| Data fragmentation | Entity IDs, master data, and histories do not join cleanly | Legal, procurement, healthcare, supply chain, public records |
| Causal confusion | Optimization uses correlations that do not represent incremental impact | Marketing attribution, promotions, NBA, pricing |
| False-positive cost ignored | Teams optimize recall while overloading workers or blocking good customers | Fraud, AML, security, quality, support escalation |
| Workflow integration missing | Predictions stay in dashboards instead of systems of action | Supply planning, AP/AR, AIOps, collections, field dispatch |
| Human trust and incentives | Users override or ignore outputs because incentives, explanations, and controls are weak | Sales, finance close, manufacturing, legal, healthcare |
| Governance and risk gaps | Privacy, fairness, model drift, documentation, or auditability are not designed upfront | HR, healthcare, financial services, public sector, security |
| Over-automation | Automating exceptions or regulated decisions without safe human review creates operational or legal risk | Prior authorization, claims, lending, sanctions, legal review |
